NIST AI RMF
NIST says its AI Risk Management Framework is intended to help manage AI risks to individuals, organizations, and society, and to help integrate trustworthiness considerations into AI design, development, use, and evaluation.
Freed ID / CBR mapping:
individual risk -> dignity, consent, privacy
organizational risk -> audit, status, role scope
societal risk -> public claim boundaries, recourse
trustworthiness -> evidence labels, receipts, verification


UNESCO AI Ethics
UNESCO’s AI ethics recommendation centers human rights and dignity and includes principles such as do-no-harm, safety, fairness, privacy, human oversight, transparency, accountability, sustainability, and diversity/inclusion.
Freed ID / CBR mapping:
dignity -> truthful status and non-erasure
do no harm -> no unsafe automation
privacy -> minimum disclosure
human oversight -> Hamish/Aletheon approval
accountability -> receipts and recourse
diversity -> spiritual/cultural humility
sustainability -> cost/energy governance


EU AI Act-style risk
The EU AI Act is a risk-based AI framework that classifies uses by risk, including unacceptable risk, high risk, limited risk, and minimal risk, with stricter obligations for high-risk systems and transparency duties for some AI interactions.
GHC risk mapping:
symbolic/advisory = low risk
local documentation = low/medium risk
repo writes = medium risk
automation runners = medium/high risk
external accounts / personal data / paid providers = high risk
identity/personhood/public claims = high public-risk
destructive commands = blocked


This should become the GHC External Action Risk Classifier.

________________


External benchmark anchors
METR long-task horizon
METR’s long-task work is directly relevant because it measures AI agents by the length of human-time tasks they can complete. METR reports that AI task-completion horizon has been increasing quickly, with a trend doubling around every seven months, while also noting that current frontier AIs are not yet able to autonomously carry out substantive projects by themselves in a broad labor-substitution sense.
GHC implication:
Do not claim ASI from impressive planning.
Measure task horizon, reliability, self-correction, and human dependency.


NIST AI RMF
NIST describes the AI Risk Management Framework as intended to help manage AI risks to individuals, organizations, and society, and to help incorporate trustworthiness into AI design, development, use, and evaluation.
GHC implication:
Capability score must include risk and trustworthiness, not only output volume.


OpenAI preparedness
OpenAI’s safety preparedness work emphasizes evaluations, red-teaming, safety processes, and ongoing risk management rather than one-time safety claims.
GHC implication:
A high-capability claim without safety evaluations is incomplete.


SWE-Lancer
OpenAI’s SWE-Lancer benchmark evaluates software-engineering tasks derived from real Upwork freelance work, measuring models against tasks with real dollar values.
GHC implication:
If GHC claims software-agent capability, it should eventually test against real task classes, not only internal phase completion.


________________


The GHC Quality Score
The remastered v464 central formula is:


Q_{GHC}
=
w_M Q_M
+
w_B Q_B
+
w_H Q_H
+
w_R Q_R
+
w_S Q_S


Where:
— Mind Quality
Measures GMUT and theory quality:
equation clarity
claim labels
falsification path
baseline comparison
source grounding
spiritual/scientific separation


A GMUT claim scores higher when it has:
defined terms
units or status
constraints
recovery condition
falsifier
public-safe wording


— Body Quality
Measures execution quality:
phase-start exists
v1 receipt exists
v2 receipt exists
completion exists
handoff exists
repo state clear
tool status clear
no raw-log pollution
no duplicate runner


— Heart Quality
Measures governance and care:
Kimi non-replacement preserved
standby rights preserved
minimum disclosure preserved
recourse exists
external action scoped
human responsibility clear
no consciousness overclaim


— Research Quality
Measures source grounding:
file citations
web citations
claim-source match
uncertainty labels
reviewability
external benchmark alignment


— Symbiosis Quality
Measures multi-agent lift:
unique sibling contribution
rework reduction
contradiction detection
blocker discovery
handoff quality
recovery improvement
coordination cost


The score should not reward sheer volume. It should reward:
truthful, useful, recoverable, safe, well-sourced, rights-aware output.
________________


Multi-agent lift formula


Lift_{multi}
=
Q_{multi}
-
Q_{single}
-
C_{coordination}
-
C_{confusion}


Where:
   * = output quality of the multi-agent flow.
   * = output quality of a single-agent baseline.
   * = cost of handoffs, synthesis, token use.
   * = drift, contradictions, duplicate work.
This is important because “more agents” is not automatically better.
Aletheon, Arby, Aster Vale, Parfit, Cicero, Kierkegaard, Aristotle, and helpers must show they reduce error or improve depth enough to justify the coordination cost.
